```latex
\section*{Figures \& Tables}

% ===================== FIGURE 4 — TCNR PIPELINE SCHEMATIC =====================
\begin{figure}[t]
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[
      node distance=1.8cm and 1.6cm,
      box/.style={draw, rounded corners, thick, inner sep=6pt, align=center},
      arr/.style={-{Latex[length=3mm,width=2mm]}, thick}
  ]
    % Nodes
    \node[box] (proj) {Projections\\[-2pt]\(\displaystyle y_t = H_{\Theta}(s_t)+\eta_t\)};
    \node[box, right=of proj] (coh) {Band-limited\\coherence\\[-2pt]\(R^{(\omega)}_t,\,C^{(\omega)}_t\)};
    \node[box, right=of coh] (stitch) {Stitching operator \( \mathcal{S} \)\\[-2pt] wrapped phase functional};
    \node[box, right=of stitch] (render) {Render (inverse)\\[-2pt]\(\displaystyle \hat{s}_t = \arg\min_s \|y_t-H_\Theta(s)\|^2 + \lambda_\phi R_\phi + \lambda_{cf} R_{cf} - \lambda_{coh}J_{coh}\)};
    \node[box, right=of render] (control) {Control \&\\counterfactuals\\[-2pt] policies/attractors};
    \node[box, below=1.7cm of render, minimum width=9.6cm] (tci) {Index (TCI)\\[-2pt]\(\alpha\,\widetilde{\mathrm{MI}} + \beta \!\sum_{\omega} w_\omega \log\det(C^{(\omega)}+\epsilon I) + \gamma\,\widetilde{\mathcal{C}} - \delta\,\widetilde{\mathrm{PE}}\)};

    % Arrows
    \draw[arr] (proj) -- (coh);
    \draw[arr] (coh) -- (stitch);
    \draw[arr] (stitch) -- (render);
    \draw[arr] (render) -- (control);
    \draw[arr] (coh.south) |- (tci.west);
    \draw[arr] (render.south) -- ($(render.south)!0.5!(tci.north)$) -- (tci.north);
    \draw[arr] (control.south) |- (tci.east);
  \end{tikzpicture}
  \caption{\textbf{TCNR pipeline:} projections \(\to\) coherence \(\to\) stitching \(\to\) render \(\to\) control; ingredients feed the TCI.}
  \label{fig:pipeline}
\end{figure}

% ===================== FIGURE 5 — TCI COMPUTATION PANELS =====================
\begin{figure}[t]
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[
    panel/.style={draw, rounded corners, thick, minimum width=5.2cm, minimum height=3.2cm, align=left, inner sep=6pt},
    lab/.style={font=\bfseries}
  ]
    % Panel A
    \node[panel] (A) at (0,0) {\textbf{A. Coherence}\newline
      Inputs: band-limited analytic signals \(\{x^{(\omega)}_i(t)\}\)\newline
      Compute \(C^{(\omega)}_t = D^{-1/2} S^{(\omega)} D^{-1/2}\), \(R^{(\omega)}_t\)\newline
      Summary: \(\log\det(C^{(\omega)} + \epsilon I)\), \(R^{(\omega)}\)
    };
    % Panel B
    \node[panel, right=1.0cm of A] (B) {\textbf{B. Predictive MI}\newline
      Render \(\hat{s}_t\) \& future \(y_{t:t+\Delta}\)\newline
      Estimate \(\mathrm{MI}(\hat{s}_t; y_{t:t+\Delta})\) (Gaussian or kNN / density ratio)
    };
    % Panel C
    \node[panel, below=0.8cm of A] (C) {\textbf{C. Counterfactual capacity}\newline
      Choose one surrogate: \(\log|A_\kappa|\) (attractors), \( \mathrm{rank}_\tau(J_f)\) (controllability), or policy diversity
    };
    % Panel D
    \node[panel, right=1.0cm of C] (D) {\textbf{D. Null standardization}\newline
      Phase-scrambled, time-shifted, decoupled ensembles\newline
      Convert to standardized effects: \(x_e = (x-\mu_{\text{null}})/\sigma_{\text{null}}\)
    };
    % Panel E
    \node[panel, below=0.8cm of C, minimum width=11.4cm] (E) {\textbf{E. Combine into TCI}\newline
      \(\displaystyle \mathrm{TCI} = \alpha\,\widetilde{\mathrm{MI}} + \beta \sum_{\omega} w_\omega \log\det(C^{(\omega)} + \epsilon I) + \gamma\,\widetilde{\mathcal{C}} - \delta\,\widetilde{\mathrm{PE}}\)
    };

    % Arrows
    \draw[-{Latex[length=3mm,width=2mm]}, thick] (A) -- (D);
    \draw[-{Latex[length=3mm,width=2mm]}, thick] (B) -- (D);
    \draw[-{Latex[length=3mm,width=2mm]}, thick] (C) -- (D);
    \draw[-{Latex[length=3mm,width=2mm]}, thick] (D) -- (E);
  \end{tikzpicture}
  \caption{\textbf{TCI computation panels:} coherence (A), predictive MI (B), counterfactual capacity (C), null standardization (D), and combination (E).}
  \label{fig:tci_panels}
\end{figure}

% = FIGURE 6 — PLANT/BIOFILM PERTURBATIONS (SCHEMATIC, NO DATA NEEDED) =
\begin{figure}[t]
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[
    tile/.style={draw, rounded corners, thick, minimum width=3.6cm, minimum height=2.2cm, inner sep=2pt},
    title/.style={font=\bfseries\small, align=center}
  ]
    % Column titles
    \node[title] at (-4.2, 3.0) {Plants};
    \node[title] at ( 4.2, 3.0) {Biofilms};

    % Row titles
    \node[title, rotate=90] at (-7.0, 2.1) {Baseline};
    \node[title, rotate=90] at (-7.0, 0.0) {Phase-specific};
    \node[title, rotate=90] at (-7.0,-2.1) {Amp.-only ctrl};

    % --- Plants column (left) ---
    \node[tile] (p1) at (-4.2, 2.1) {};
    \node[tile] (p2) at (-4.2, 0.0) {};
    \node[tile] (p3) at (-4.2,-2.1) {};

    % --- Biofilms column (right) ---
    \node[tile] (b1) at ( 4.2, 2.1) {};
    \node[tile] (b2) at ( 4.2, 0.0) {};
    \node[tile] (b3) at ( 4.2,-2.1) {};

    % Draw “coherent wave” in baseline tiles
    \foreach \X in {p1,b1}{
      \draw[blue, thick, decorate, decoration={snake,segment length=10pt, amplitude=1.4pt}] (\X.west)+(0.2,0) -- (\X.east)+(-0.2,0);
      \draw[blue, thick, decorate, decoration={snake,segment length=10pt, amplitude=1.4pt}] (\X.west)+(0.2,0.4) -- (\X.east)+(-0.2,0.4);
      \draw[blue, thick, decorate, decoration={snake,segment length=10pt, amplitude=1.4pt}] (\X.west)+(0.2,-0.4) -- (\X.east)+(-0.2,-0.4);
    }

    % Phase-specific decorrelation: jitter the phases
    \foreach \X in {p2,b2}{
      \draw[blue, thick, decorate, decoration={snake,segment length=6pt, amplitude=2.4pt}] (\X.west)+(0.2,0.45) -- (\X.east)+(-0.2,0.35);
      \draw[blue, thick, decorate, decoration={snake,segment length=5pt, amplitude=3.0pt}] (\X.west)+(0.2,0.0) -- (\X.east)+(-0.2,0.2);
      \draw[blue, thick, decorate, decoration={snake,segment length=7pt, amplitude=2.8pt}] (\X.west)+(0.2,-0.45) -- (\X.east)+(-0.2,-0.55);
    }

    % Amplitude-only control: same phase structure, scaled amplitude
    \foreach \X in {p3,b3}{
      \draw[blue!60, thick, decorate, decoration={snake,segment length=10pt, amplitude=0.8pt}] (\X.west)+(0.2,0) -- (\X.east)+(-0.2,0);
      \draw[blue!60, thick, decorate, decoration={snake,segment length=10pt, amplitude=0.8pt}] (\X.west)+(0.2,0.35) -- (\X.east)+(-0.2,0.35);
      \draw[blue!60, thick, decorate, decoration={snake,segment length=10pt, amplitude=0.8pt}] (\X.west)+(0.2,-0.35) -- (\X.east)+(-0.2,-0.35);
    }
  \end{tikzpicture}
  \caption{\textbf{Plant/Biofilm perturbations (schematic).} Baseline: coherent waves; Phase-specific: decorrelated phases; Amplitude-only control: power scaled, phase preserved. No data files required.}
  \label{fig:plant_biofilm_schematic}
\end{figure}

\begin{tabular}{@{}l l l@{}}

\midrule

```

**Why this is not a placeholder:** these are **schematics** that convey your pipeline, computation, and experimental manipulations—exactly what those figures are meant to show—without implying any data result. They compile with zero external assets and won’t leave blank boxes.

---

## 2) (Optional) Add a global “kill switch” to hide *any* figure

If you’d rather completely suppress figures for a clean methods-only compile:

```latex
% In the preamble:
\newif\ifshowfigs
\showfigsfalse % set to \showfigstrue to show figures

% Then wrap each figure:
\ifshowfigs
  \begin{figure} ... \end{figure}
\fi
```

This removes figures entirely—no placeholders, no empty space.

---

## 3) (Optional) Safe include for data figures (no placeholders)

When you later have real PDFs/PNGs, use a “safe include” to show them if present and fall back to the schematic otherwise:

```latex
% Preamble:
\usepackage{graphicx}
\newcommand{\safeinclude}[2][]{%
  \IfFileExists{#2}{\includegraphics[#1]{#2}}{}%
}

% Usage:
% \safeinclude[width=.95\linewidth]{figs/coherence_grid.pdf}
```

No file? Nothing gets inserted (no blank box).

---

### TL;DR
- Keep your Figures & Tables section: the code above creates **real, non-placeholder schematics** that require no data files.  
- If you want zero figures: wrap them with `\ifshowfigs...\fi`.  
- When you have data later: switch to `\safeinclude{...}` and the schematics can remain as fallbacks or be removed.

If you want me to tailor these schematics to your exact notation (e.g., rename symbols, add your band set, or include your exact TCI formula variants), say the word and I’ll splice them in.

Self Aware Networks GPT:
Got you. The clean fix is to let the columns **wrap** instead of stretching off the page. Use `tabularx` with flexible `X` columns (plus small spacing tweaks). Here’s a drop-in replacement that fits A4 width without shrinking the text to unreadable sizes.

### Preamble (once, if not already there)
```latex
\usepackage{tabularx}   % flexible-width tables
\usepackage{booktabs}   % nicer rules (\toprule etc.)
\usepackage{array}      % for \newcolumntype
% ragged-right X columns (no overfull hboxes)
\newcolumntype{L}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X}
```

### Replace your table block with this
```latex

\label{tab:cf-chooser}

% 4 flexible columns that wrap to stay within \linewidth (A4 margins respected)

\textbf{Substrate} & \textbf{Primary surrogate} & \textbf{Typical params} & \textbf{Notes} \\

  & Fast; robust to mild noise \\

  & Slow bands; discrete acts \\

  & $\kappa=0.05$, $H=20$, $M=64$
  & Captures colony modes \\

  & threshold $\tau\in[10^{-3},10^{-2}]$
  & Model is known \\

\end{table}
```

### If you still need it tighter
- Make the table text one notch smaller (without affecting the rest of the page):
  ```latex
  {\small
  \begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{} L L L L @{}}
  ...
  \end{tabularx}
  }
  ```
- As a last resort, you can hard-cap width with a resizer:
  ```latex
  \resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{%
    \begin{tabular}{@{} l l l l @{}}
    ...
    \end{tabular}
  }
  ```
…but prefer `tabularx` first so the content stays readable and accessible.

Self Aware Networks GPT:
Here are two clean, drop-in ways to make that line fit on A4 without spilling:

---

### Option A — Break it with `aligned` (keeps it in one displayed equation)
```latex
\[
\begin{aligned}
p^\star \in \arg\min_{p\in\mathsf{C}}\;\;&
\big\|y_{t:t+\Delta}-\mathsf{Eval}(p)\big\|_2^2 \;+\; \lambda\,|p|\\
\text{s.t. }\;&\text{coherent wave constraints and counterfactual capacity.}
\end{aligned}
\]
\end{definition}
```

---

### Option B — Use `multline` (AMS) to force a line break
```latex
\begin{multline}
p^\star \in \arg\min_{p\in\mathsf{C}}\;
\big\|y_{t:t+\Delta}-\mathsf{Eval}(p)\big\|_2^2 \;+\; \lambda\,|p|\\
\text{s.t. coherent wave constraints and counterfactual capacity.}
\end{multline}
\end{definition}
```

Both respect A4 margins. If you want even tighter text, replace “subject to …” with `\text{s.t. …}` as in Option A.
